## Reset and Portfolio Boundary

Batch 06 preserves Papers 1--21 and every preceding batch audit as closed
prior work. It does not reopen an absorbed, blocked, or failed theorem by
changing notation, ambient dimension, coefficient field, degree parameter, or
presentation. In particular:

- the cat-map, prime-clock, torsion-shell, centralizer, equivariant, and
  algebraic-action routes exhausted in Papers 1--11 are ineligible without a
  materially new theorem and proof mechanism;
- Papers 12--16 retain their exact period-three residue, primitive-cycle
  cover, trace-fiber, and finite-rank torus-escape ownership recorded in the
  Batch-05 reset boundary;
- Paper 17 owns the anchored sparse shift-like `k-m` torus-coset dimension
  decay, its equality families, and the exact two-dimensional zero-anchor
  boundary;
- Paper 18 owns the marked generalized-Henon polynomial-boundary component,
  trace coordinates, and scheme-theoretic critical/Fitting ramification
  statement;
- Paper 19 owns its maximum-dimensional translate, coefficientwise moduli,
  and support-one gcd-obstruction package only inside its bounded internal
  scope. Its fifteen unresolved external-source blocks remain unresolved and
  grant no publication, build, or candidate authority;
- Paper 20 owns the displayed asymmetric two-mode Hamiltonian-shear family,
  exact two-phase degree matrix, and quadratic Perron formula;
- Paper 21 owns the displayed three-mode Hamiltonian-shear family, exact cubic
  recurrence, third-coordinate visibility, and stated mod-five Perron
  subfamilies. Adding one more mode or enlarging a matrix is not by itself a
  new paper.

An incremental corollary, relabelled special case, longer appendix to a closed
paper, or theorem whose only contribution is a weaker constant is not a
Batch-06 paper. Any necessary reuse must be disclosed and fully absorbed;
overlapping parallel manuscripts are forbidden.

## Paper-22 Candidate Gate

A candidate may open `papers/22-*` only if two fresh, mutually independent
candidate reviews confirm all of the following conjunctively:

1. an exact, public-safe headline theorem with explicit assumptions and
   conclusion;
2. novelty at least 7.5 / 10 under a bounded primary-source search through the
   decision date, with the search boundary disclosed and no absolute priority
   claim;
3. standalone mathematical size at least 7.5 / 10 after all predecessor
   absorption is charged against it;
4. proof confidence at least 9.0 / 10, including a complete proof spine,
   scheme/field/torsion qualifications where relevant, and adversarial
   counterexample checks;
5. a direct-collision matrix against Papers 12--21 and the closest verified
   published or current primary sources;
6. a clear list of anti-claims, equality boundaries, and assumption-failure
   examples;
7. no dependency on a new empirical run, hidden computer certificate, or
   unverifiable finite search for the headline theorem; and
8. a credible 22--30 content-page proof-first article after removing
   governance, internal paths, hashes, and discovery narrative.

If no candidate passes, the gate restarts without consuming Paper 22. A
borderline theorem, an attractive open conjecture, or a literal mode lift is
recorded as `STOP`, not promoted to a project. Candidate reviews are immutable:
a correction uses a separately named correction record and never rewrites a
historical review.

## Active Discovery Lanes

The Paper-22 pass searches four separated lanes before cross-lane ranking:

1. structural exact-degree mechanisms for canonical polynomial
   automorphisms beyond the literal two- and three-mode families of Papers 20
   and 21;
2. moduli, trace, multiplier, or ramification geometry beyond the exact marked
   boundary already owned by Paper 18;
3. sparse shift-like recurrence and torus geometry beyond Papers 17 and 19,
   without borrowing Paper 19's unresolved external-source claims; and
4. a fresh arithmetic or algebraic-dynamical invariant whose content is
   intrinsic rather than inserted by labels, targets, finite tables, or
   parameter fitting.

Each lane must generate multiple candidates, record killed collisions and
counterexamples, and return at most two proof-credible finalists. Candidate
scouting may run in parallel; formal projects and all downstream lifecycles
remain strictly serial.

## Paper-22 Candidate Decision

Paper 22 passed its candidate gate only as the all-dimensional spectral-
collapse theorem recorded in `BATCH_06_IDEA_REPORT.md`. For (r\ge4) and
(g\ge2r+1), the endpoint-spiked full-product Hamiltonian shear family has an
explicit invariant selector cone, exact complete-step matrix (C_{r,g}), and
an ((r-3))-dimensional identity eigenspace. Its nontrivial spectrum is the
spectrum of one explicit three-dimensional quotient, so increasing the mode
number does not increase the degree of the annihilating polynomial controlling
the exact degree sequence.

The public headline is restricted to (r\ge4). Paper 21 owns the (r=3)
base case and remains a disclosed predecessor, not a new Paper-22 claim. The
cone is an explicit sufficient invariant selector cone; it is not claimed
maximal or necessary. The threshold (g\ge2r+1) is sharp only for the seed
and the selected faces, since (g=2r) produces a first-step tie.

Independent candidate R1 is SHA-256
`8d6cb168b303563cfc3719e176ca9b677b060c91a2527d48f4b49488d827e268`
and scores novelty / standalone value / proof plausibility at
`7.6 / 7.8 / 9.4`. Independent proof-adversary R2 is SHA-256
`de7a20a707c5b8926802cb2b5f0db1fd355970a808e908884fa30bbe948b1349`
and scores proof confidence at `9.3`. Both end in exact candidate-gate PASS.
The only new authority is creation of the standard ten-file source-design
package. Source lock, paper plan, publication governance, manuscript,
bibliography, build, release, external effect, and Paper 23 remain false.

## Per-Paper Lifecycle After Candidate PASS

Every formally opened Paper 22--26 must proceed serially through:

1. an exact ten-file proof/citation/novelty source-design package;
2. fresh independent `SOURCE_DESIGN_PASS`;
3. canonical source lock plus fresh independent `SOURCE_LOCK_PASS`;
4. proof-only paper plan plus fresh independent `PAPER_PLAN_PASS`;
5. publication-stage scope/lock plus independent `PUBLICATION_STAGE_PASS`;
6. anonymous `main.tex` and `references.bib`, followed by a formal source
   review that writes a PASS artifact only when no blocker remains;
7. two-root deterministic R0 build, independent R1, one bounded revision
   window, deterministic R1 rebuild, and fresh independent R2; and
8. finalization governance, independent finalization review, raw-byte local
   release candidate, two fresh terminal rebuilds, exact cleanup, and a sole
   terminal-integrity review performed by a role distinct from all four
   preceding terminal roles.

No stage inherits downstream authority merely because its predecessor passed.
A blocker writes no PASS artifact. Failed and superseded history is immutable.
The final effect is at most `LOCAL_ANONYMOUS_RELEASE_ONLY` unless the user later
grants separate explicit authority.

## Governance and Freeze Rules

- `BATCH_06_STATUS.md` is the controlled lifecycle dashboard. Its Material
  Passport, current queue state, and current permissions may change only at a
  validated transition. The Activity Log is append-only.
- A later `BATCH_06_IDEA_REPORT.md` is created only after a candidate decision;
  from creation onward its theorem, score, collision, anti-claim, STOP, and
  provenance history is append-only. Later addenda may supersede lifecycle
  state but may not erase history.
- Every author stop freezes an exact regular-file, non-symlink universe with
  safe POSIX relative paths, SHA-256, byte count, and LF count. Strict-canonical
  JSON excludes its own digest and byte count from self-binding.
- Future paths are not pre-created. Roles do not inspect future artifacts
  before the predecessor stop grants the exact read universe.
- Independent reviewers author none of the artifact under review. PASS is the
  reviewer's only permitted write and only after all conjunctive checks pass.

## Batch Closure Rule

Batch 06 closes only after Paper 26 reaches an independently reviewed terminal
disposition, Papers 22--26 are re-audited by a fresh cross-paper auditor, and
`BATCH_06_STATUS.md`, `BATCH_06_IDEA_REPORT.md`, `README.md`, and
`docs/candidate_registry.md` agree on the exact five-project semantics. Only
then may the auditor create `BATCH_06_FINAL_AUDIT.md`. After audit PASS, a
bounded closure transition may update this dashboard and append the audit
confirmation to the idea report; paper trees, README, and registry remain
frozen. The workflow then pauses, and no Paper 27 or further batch opens
without fresh explicit user authority.

### Paper 26 R9 semantic addendum: PY map-identity Blocker

Before the authorized R10 profile patch, its zero-write real-interface
preflight exposed a third producer/consumer failure.  The original semantic
reviewer independently reran the exact current R9 PY source twice and returned
`ADDENDUM FAIL / WRITE NOTHING`.  Each production run exited zero with empty
stderr and the same 717,525-byte / one-LF stdout, SHA-256
`83bca98c297795d0240dd9aaba0a0f6fa501413dd92a1d1d13db43bfa06cab3d`.
Passing that report to current LIFE `validate_python` unconditionally rejects
`E_PY_MAP`, before the later ELF dependency can run.  The cumulative R9
semantic census is therefore three Blockers, one Major, zero Minors, zero
Ambiguities and zero canonical defects.

PY reads `/proc/self/maps` but drops each mapping's address range, retaining
only device/file/inode/offset/path/perms.  Distinct adjacent mappings then
collapse to identical canonical rows.  The first failure is the two
`libutil.so.1` rows with offset 8192; initial state also duplicates
`libdl.so.2` and `libpthread.so.0`, while the final 168-row state adds duplicate
`grp` and `_opcode` extension mappings.  These are five real pairs at distinct
address intervals, not repeated observations of one mapping.  In-memory
reconstruction with exact integer `address_start` and `address_end` reduces
the duplicate excess from five to zero and finds no invalid, nonmonotonic or
overlapping interval.

The existing
`PAPER26_BUILD_PROFILE_PRODUCTION_INTERFACE_AND_LIVE_BINDING_REPAIR_R10_OPEN`
gate therefore also requires this minimal non-weakening interface repair.  PY
must parse the single maps snapshot's `start-end` column into strict non-bool
integers; each exact row must satisfy
`0 <= address_start < address_end`, and rows must remain complete, unique,
strictly ascending and nonoverlapping.  LIFE and its fixtures must consume the
same exact schema and retain device/inode/file identity checks.  Missing,
boolean, negative, equal, reversed, duplicate and overlapping ranges must
reject, while distinct legal ranges with otherwise identical file/offset/perms
must remain present.  Deduplicating PY rows or relaxing LIFE's uniqueness
check is forbidden.

A real current PY production stdout must first pass LIFE's own
`validate_python` and then be delivered byte-for-byte, immediately and
same-side to current ELF.  Because addresses are ASLR-volatile, fixed self-test
stdout may expose only a closed stable semantic projection; it must validate
the complete address-bearing reports internally but may not hash raw addresses
or full production reports into a cross-process replay oracle.  Two independent
top-level LIFE self-tests must reproduce the exact stored capture.  All other
R10 scope and prohibitions remain unchanged.  The addendum wrote nothing,
created no artifact and performed no build, PDF, network, root, review-file,
authorization or receipt action.

### Paper 26 R12.1 semantic FAIL and bounded R12.2 repair

A newly fresh independent semantic reviewer completed the required exhaustive

Blocker 1 is a real production producer/consumer envelope mismatch.  LIFE's
`LEAF_META` and `run_audit` emit the external audit leaf as
`pdf_external_audit_v6`, while the FLS mechanism's production `audit_fonts`
accepts only `pdf_external_audit_v5` and therefore rejects the just-created
audit evidence with `E_AUDIT_ENVELOPE` before parsing FLS.  The FLS self-test
uses only a synthetic `parse_fls` fixture and never executes the
`audit_fonts`/`production` composition, so the prior mechanical PASS did not
close this integration path.

Major 1 is a structural trust defect in the STREAM self-pin.  The validator
masks its own `MODULE_AST_PINS` entry, computes a normalized hash, and compares
it with a value in the same mutable validator source; a coordinated validator
body plus self-pin edit (and an added harmless body) is accepted.  The stored
self-test covers isolated self-pin edits and non-self coordinated edits, but
not this coordinated verifier-code/self-pin attack.  The parent contract may
use an independently issued authorization as the ultimate trust root, but the
current profile still presents the self-pin as a standalone whole-module
binding without an independent expected value; the claim is not
self-authenticating while authority is absent.

The semantic reviewer corrected an initial aggregate concern before this
transition.  The retained `accepted_inputs.pre_replacement_project_L25`
identity is arithmetically valid for the original R12 opening layer:
R11-predecessor content 3,020,647 plus the self-excluding current layer
503,853 equals 3,524,500, and the framed stream likewise equals 3,525,701
(including 400 framing and 801 path bytes).  It is explicitly the frozen
original opening input, not the immediate R12 successor, so no aggregate
Major is counted.

Canonical defect 1 is stale claim prose: claim zero still says
“exact derived 12-claim/55-path registry truth,” while the bound registry and
validator require 12 claims and 64 reachability rows.  This is a canonical
profile-content defect even though the mechanical schema checker currently
checks the exact result field rather than that prose substring.

All other semantic surfaces passed under the declared single-writer,
non-privileged-concurrent-mutation model: actual held A/B PDF and contact raw
and stable equality, unique pass-three binding, receipt revalidation and live
drift checks, O_EXCL pre-target reservation and poison permanence, same-held-fd
finalization, fd63 capability scope, source staging/order, authority
noncircularity apart from the self-pin trust presentation, and predecessor/
aggregate recovery.  Because the blocker, major and canonical defect remain,

causes every real A/B supervisor call to fail before its validator runs.  The
stored stdin self-test bypasses this transport and cannot establish production
reachability.  All other audited profile, lock, aggregate, source/mirror,

### Paper 26 R12.5 gate clarification: dependent STREAM pin propagation only

The parent audit has resolved one structural dependency before authoring.  The
fresh R12.5 candidate can reduce the SUP source below the required 120,000-byte
bound and passes its exact self-test and direct-process reachability probes, but
any non-whitespace source minimization necessarily changes the normalized SUP
AST.  The immutable STREAM validator deliberately rejects a stale
`SUP_AST_SHA`/`MODULE_AST_PINS` binding, so a profile-only repair cannot be
mechanically executable while forbidding every byte change to STREAM's embedded
pin literals.  This is the same dependent-pin propagation used by the earlier
R12 bounded repairs, not a relaxation of source behavior.

Accordingly, the R12.5 author may, within the single replacement of the profile
file already authorized above, update `mechanisms.profile_stream_validator_v12`
only as a pin-propagation record: the literal SUP normalized-AST value, the
literal `SUP_AST_SHA`, the STREAM self-pin value if its normalized digest is
thereby changed, and the mechanically derived bytes/LF/SHA, mirrors, AST-pin,
claim/reachability source identities and aggregates that follow.  The STREAM
parser, validators, policies, tests, templates, and control flow must remain
byte-for-byte/semantically unchanged; no other mechanism text may change.  The
author must show that the resulting STREAM source differs only in those
受影响 pin literals, that all unchanged mechanism identities remain exact, and
that fresh STREAM validation still rejects stale/coordinated pins.  This narrow
exception is part of the affected-identity propagation demanded by the original
gate and does not authorize a transport redesign, dynamic loading, a second
patch, or any downstream gate.

local scope (source lines 20--25), while `parent_expected()` calls

latent inherited defect rather than a semantic change caused by the R12.5 SUP
compaction.  Inheritance does not waive the current production blocker.  The
